## File: docs/research/2026-rewrite-efficacy-prereg.md
````markdown
  1: # Pre-registration — Does patina rewrite actually reduce AI-likeness?
  2: 
  3: This is a plan, written down on 2026-07-10, before any of the data existed. The
  4: hypotheses, the metrics, and the rules for calling the thing a success or a
  5: failure are all fixed here so that none of them can quietly move once the numbers
  6: come back. Results go somewhere else — `2026-rewrite-efficacy.md`. Nothing below
  7: gets edited to match what we find. Deviations get appended, dated, and explained.
  8: 
  9: > Framing (per `docs/ROADMAP.md`): patina is an **AI-likeness humanizer**, not a
 10: > detector-bypass product. "Efficacy" here means *reducing perceived AI-likeness
 11: > while preserving meaning*, not defeating any specific detector. We borrow the
 12: > measurement axes of TH-Bench (evasion/quality/overhead) but reframe axis 1 as
 13: > *perceived humanness*, not adversarial evasion.
 14: 
 15: ## Background & prior art
 16: 
 17: - Detection is already measured (`docs/research/2026-rebaseline.md`): overall AI
 18:   catch 67.3% [63.5–71.0%], with a known weak cell (ko GPT-family 44%). What is
 19:   **not** measured: whether the *rewrite* pass reduces AI-likeness on an
 20:   independent yardstick. This program fills that gap.
 21: - **LLM-judge self-preference bias** (arXiv:2410.21819): LLM judges over-rate
 22:   low-perplexity / same-family text. Mitigation = cross-family judging + direct
 23:   scoring over pairwise "pick the best." → We never let a rewriter's own family
 24:   be its sole judge; we score each text independently and shuffle order.
 25: - **Humanizer benchmarks / paraphrase attacks** (TH-Bench arXiv:2503.08708;
 26:   DIPPER arXiv:2303.13408): consistent finding of an *evasion ↔ quality ↔ cost*
 27:   trade-off — no tool wins all three. → We measure meaning preservation and
 28:   human-control over-editing alongside any AI-likeness drop, so a "win" that
 29:   guts meaning is caught.
 30: - **Human perception cues** (arXiv:2505.01877; 2510.05136): humans key on
 31:   sentence-length variability, vocabulary range, redundancy/repetition, and
 32:   cultural/historical flattening. → These inform the mechanism regression (RQ3)
 33:   feature set.
 34: - **Korean-specific** (KatFish/KatFishNet, ACL 2025, arXiv:2503.00032): spacing,
 35:   POS-combination, punctuation are the discriminative Korean signals. → ko
 36:   mechanism features align to KatFish axes; our `katfish-calibration.mjs` is the
 37:   bridge.
 38: 
 39: ## Research questions & hypotheses
 40: 
 41: **RQ1 — Construct validity.** Run this first. Everything downstream depends on
 42: whether "AI-likeness" even holds still when different raters look at it.
 43: - H1: pairwise agreement (Spearman ρ / Krippendorff's α) among the three
 44:   cross-family LLM judges, the deterministic stylometry score, and the patina
 45:   internal score is > 0 and materially positive on the pilot set.
 46: - **Decision rule:** if inter-judge α < 0.4 on the pilot, STOP the main study and
 47:   redesign the instrument (the yardstick is too noisy to trust any efficacy
 48:   claim). Report the failure rather than proceeding.
 49: 
 50: **RQ2 — Perceptual efficacy.** The primary question, and the one patina exists
 51: to answer: does a rewrite read less like a machine to someone who wasn't told?
 52: - H2a: mean independent-judge AI-likeness(rewrite) < AI-likeness(original AI),
 53:   reported as paired effect size (Cliff's δ) + 95% bootstrap CI.
 54: - H2b: in a shuffled 3-way blind (original-AI / rewrite / real-human), the rate
 55:   at which the rewrite is labelled "AI" is < half the original's rate.
 56: - **Anti-circularity decision rule:** if the patina internal-score drop is large
 57:   but the independent-judge drop is not (Δinternal − Δjudge exceeds the pilot's
 58:   agreement band), we conclude patina is **gaming its own detector** and flag the
 59:   rewrite pipeline for redesign — a headline finding, not a footnote.
 60: 
 61: **RQ3 — Mechanism.** Suppose the needle moves. What actually moved it?
 62: - Regress per-text judge-score delta on feature deltas (burstiness /
 63:   sentence-length variance, ending-suffix monotony [ko], type-token / MATTR,
 64:   lexicon-marker count, patina pattern-hit count, ko: spacing & punctuation per
 65:   KatFish). Report standardized coefficients.
 66: - Output: ranked list separating *perceptually load-bearing* tells from
 67:   *detector-only* tells → evidence-based pattern-pack priorities.
 68: 
 69: **RQ4 — Humanizer fingerprint.** Barely studied, and the one that would embarrass
 70: us most: a tool that scrubs every text into the *same* voice has not removed the
 71: machine, it has replaced one machine with another.
 72: - H4: mean pairwise stylistic similarity among *rewrites* vs among *human
 73:   controls*. If rewrites cluster tighter than humans, the humanizer leaves its
 74:   own signature (a second-order AI tell). Descriptive + permutation test.
 75: 
 76: **RQ5 — Collateral (quality/meaning axis).**
 77: - H5a: MPS & fidelity ≥ 70 on ≥ 95% of rewrites (meaning preserved).
 78: - H5b: running rewrite on **human** controls does not push judge-rated writing
 79:   quality down beyond a pre-set churn/quality band (over-editing risk = real
 80:   usage failure).
 81: 
 82: ## Design
 83: 
 84: Two-stage, pre-registered.
 85: 
 86: - **Study 0 (pilot):** ko + en, 10 AI + 5 human each (30 texts). Judges: 2
 87:   cross-family. Purpose: validate the pipeline, estimate inter-judge agreement
 88:   (RQ1) and Δ variance for a power calc. Gate for the main study.
 89: - **Study 1 (main):** launched only if Study 0 passes RQ1. Size set by the
 90:   pilot's observed variance; target ~110 AI (ko/en primary, 3 model families ×
 91:   registers; zh/ja reduced cells) + ~55 human controls. Judges: 3 cross-family.
 92: 
 93: Corpus: reuse the labelled rebaseline intake (`artifacts/rebaseline-2025/`,
 94: ko/en 130 each, stratified by model_family × register) + human controls; raw
 95: text stays gitignored, only hashes/metadata/scores are committed.
 96: 
 97: ### Anti-circularity & bias controls
 98: 1. Cross-family judging only; a generator family never its own sole judge.
 99: 2. Judges score each text **independently** (0–100), blind to condition and to
100:    whether patina produced it; 3-way identity task uses shuffled order.
101: 3. Two judge sub-tasks kept separate: "is this AI-written?" vs "which do you
102:    prefer?" — so self-preference/perplexity bias is *measured*, not conflated
103:    with the efficacy signal.
104: 4. patina internal score is reported as a **sanity axis only**, never the
105:    primary efficacy metric (it is the rewriter's optimization target).
106: 5. Human anchor: maintainer blind-rates ~15 ko pairs; judge↔human concordance
107:    sets the trust ceiling on the LLM judges (issue #159 pilot; RQ1 cross-check).
108: 
109: ## Metrics (fixed)
110: - Primary: independent-judge AI-likeness Δ (Cliff's δ + 95% bootstrap CI);
111:   3-way "AI"-label rate for original vs rewrite.
112: - Agreement: Spearman ρ + Krippendorff's α across raters (RQ1).
113: - Meaning: MPS, fidelity, dropped-number guard hit-rate.
114: - Collateral: edit churn; human-control judge-quality Δ.
115: - Mechanism: standardized regression coefficients (RQ3).
116: - Fingerprint: rewrite-vs-human pairwise style-similarity gap (RQ4).
117: 
118: ## Success / failure criteria (pre-set)
119: - **Efficacy supported** iff H2a effect is negative with CI excluding 0 AND H2b
120:   holds AND the anti-circularity rule (RQ2) is NOT triggered AND H5a ≥ 95%.
121: - **Efficacy is a detector-gaming artifact** if the RQ2 anti-circularity rule
122:   triggers → redesign recommendation.
123: - **Instrument invalid** if RQ1 α < 0.4 → stop, redesign measurement.
124: - Every bounded cap / dropped cell / non-retry is logged; silent truncation is a
125:   protocol violation.
126: 
127: ## Token budget & footprint
128: - Heavy work runs as background scripts calling local CLIs (claude/codex/gemini);
129:   the interactive session carries only summaries.
130: - Pilot (Study 0): ~0.3–0.5M tokens across local CLI subscriptions.
131: - Main (Study 1), if greenlit by the pilot: ~3–4M tokens, spread over hours.
132: - Go/no-go on the main study is made from pilot results, not assumed up front.
133: 
134: ## Outputs
135: - `docs/research/2026-rewrite-efficacy.md` (cell tables + failure exemplars).
136: - Surviving-tell taxonomy → pattern-pack issues.
137: - Raw generations/judgments in gitignored `artifacts/`; only
138:   hashes/metadata/scores committed.
139: 
140: ## Deviations from the registered plan
141: 
142: Recorded as they happen, before the affected data is collected. The original
143: text above is not edited.
144: 
145: ### Deviation 1 (2026-07-10) — stimulus length invalidates the planned substrate
146: 
147: A 2-unit end-to-end smoke run of the pilot harness surfaced three problems with
148: using the rebaseline intake as the efficacy substrate. All are **stimulus**
149: problems, not construct problems, and would have produced an uninterpretable
150: RQ1 failure ("the instrument is invalid") when the real cause was "the passages
151: are too short to judge."
152: 
153: 1. **The corpus is paragraph-snippets, not documents.** ko AI: median 154 chars
154:    (max 207). ko human controls: median 129. en AI: median 424. No sample in
155:    `intake.*` has ≥ 3 paragraphs.
156: 2. **patina's own analyzer skips them.** `analyzeText` returns
157:    `skipReason: paragraphs<=2`, so the internal sanity axis degenerates to a
158:    1-paragraph 0-or-100 hot ratio. It cannot be compared against a graded judge
159:    score.
160: 3. **Judges disagree wildly at this length.** On one 95-char human control the
161:    two cross-family judges returned AI-likeness 24 ("human") and 75 ("ai").
162:    Snippet-level judging is dominated by noise, exactly as the human-perception
163:    literature predicts for short excerpts.
164: 
165: Additionally, the labelled ko AI snippets were rated 32 / 12 (both "human") by
166: the judges — the corpus's own AI class is not perceived as AI-like at snippet
167: length. Any "rewrite reduced AI-likeness" claim measured from that floor would
168: be meaningless.
169: 
170: **Amended design.** The pilot is restructured into three arms, and the stimulus
171: length itself becomes a measured moderator rather than an uncontrolled flaw:
172: 
173: - **Arm A — instrument validation (en, document length).** Substrate switches to
174:   the external, MIT-licensed **HAP-E** parallel corpus already present at
175:   `artifacts/rebaseline-2025/private/hape-en.private.jsonl`: 8,290 human /
176:   8,290 AI passages, **paired on `prompt_id`** (so topic *and* register are
177:   controlled by construction), 6 registers, median 2,689 (human) / 3,300 (AI)
178:   chars. This is the primary RQ1/RQ2 arm.
179: - **Arm B — stimulus-length moderator (en, snippet length).** The same judges
180:   rate the short en intake snippets (median 424 chars). Comparing inter-judge
181:   agreement α_doc (Arm A) vs α_snippet (Arm B) *quantifies* how much of the
182:   disagreement is a length artifact. This converts Deviation 1's discovery into
183:   a reportable result.
184: - **Arm C — ko, snippet length, explicitly limited.** No document-length Korean
185:   substrate exists in this repository. ko runs at snippet length and its
186:   conclusions are **interpreted only through Arm B's measured length penalty**.
187:   ko human controls are drawn from `web-human-controls.generated.private.jsonl`
188:   (250 rows, register-matched 50× each across the same 5 registers as the ko AI
189:   set) to remove the register confound present in the 25-row control file.
190: 
191: **Known limitation introduced.** HAP-E's AI side is a single 2024 model
192: (`gpt-4o-2024-08-06`), so Arm A cannot speak to modern-model AI-likeness. Arm A
193: answers "is the instrument valid and does rewrite move the needle at document
194: length"; modern-model coverage stays with the (snippet-bound) Arms B/C until a
195: document-length modern corpus exists.
196: 
197: **Blocking future work (now a named gap).** A document-length Korean corpus —
198: AI-generated across model families and human-authored controls, register-matched
199: — does not exist here. It is a prerequisite for any credible Korean rewrite
200: claim, and plausibly explains part of the weak ko cell in the detection
201: rebaseline. Filed as follow-up.
202: 
203: ### Deviation 2 (2026-07-10) — judge-response loss was silently biasing RQ1
204: 
205: Mid-pilot inspection of the first 18 judge calls found an 11% unparseable rate on
206: one judge (gemini) from two causes: a 240 s timeout on a 3.5 k-char document, and
207: **schema drift** — the judge returned a valid 0-100 rating under the key
208: `ai_status` instead of the requested `ai_likeness`, and the parser discarded it.
209: 
210: This is not a cosmetic loss. Krippendorff's alpha needs *both* judges on the same
211: passage, so every dropped rating destroys an entire agreement unit, and the drops
212: are not random: they concentrate on whatever that judge found hard to answer.
213: RQ1 — the gate for the whole program — would have been estimated on a filtered,
214: easier subset.
215: 
216: Amended before the affected data was analysed:
217: - the judge parser accepts an explicit alias set for the score key
218:   (`ai_likeness`, `ai_status`, `ai_score`, `score`, `aiLikeness`) and records
219:   which key was used (`score_key`) so drift stays visible rather than silent;
220: - it anchors on the `authorship` field and takes the last JSON object, so a judge
221:   that narrates before answering (or wraps in a code fence) still parses;
222: - each judge call gets one retry; residual failures are recorded with
223:   `retries_exhausted` and reported in the results before any effect estimate;
224: - the judge timeout rises 240 s -> 360 s for document-length passages.
225: 
226: Ratings collected under the old parser were **discarded** and all arms re-run from
227: scratch: mixing parser versions inside one dataset means inconsistent inclusion
228: criteria, which is exactly what a pre-registered protocol exists to prevent.
229: 
230: ### Deviation 3 (2026-07-10) — the gpt judge exhausted its quota mid-pilot
231: 
232: Partway through Arm A the `codex` CLI began returning
233: `ERROR: You've hit your usage limit` on stdout. The harness recorded eight cells
234: as "unparseable" without keeping the reply, so the cause was invisible until the
235: backend was probed by hand. Two fixes, both applied before the affected data was
236: analysed:
237: 
238: 1. **Panel substitution.** The primary judge panel becomes **gemini + kimi**
239:    (Moonshot). Both remain cross-family with respect to the rewriter (claude), so
240:    the self-preference control of the registered design is intact. Every passage
241:    in every arm is re-rated so the panel is uniform across A, B and C; a panel
242:    that changed halfway through would make agreement statistics meaningless.
243: 2. **The `judge-gpt` ratings already collected are kept, untouched, as a partial
244:    third rater.** They are reported separately with their coverage stated, never
245:    merged into the primary panel — mixing raters across a partially-observed cell
246:    is how a filtered subset masquerades as a complete one.
247: 
248: Two harness defects surfaced with it and are fixed:
249: - a failed judge call blanked its own reply, so a backend *quota error* was
250:   indistinguishable from a model *formatting error*. The tail of the last reply
251:   is now retained;
252: - child processes were killed individually rather than by process group, so the
253:   local CLIs' helper processes survived as orphans and blocked the next
254:   invocation of the same CLI. One reaped orphan took Arm A from 26 minutes per
255:   unit back to 2.5. Both harnesses now kill the group.
256: 
257: **Limitation this introduces.** Judge identity is confounded with time: the
258: gemini + kimi panel rated some passages minutes after the original run, and kimi
259: never saw the passages under the same conditions codex did. The pilot's purpose
260: is to size variance and validate the instrument, not to publish an effect, so the
261: confound is acceptable here and must not carry into the main study — which will
262: fix its panel up front and verify quota headroom before the first call.
263: 
264: ### Deviation 4 (2026-07-10) — the HAP-E `spok` register is not written prose
265: 
266: A rewrite failure led here, not a look at the effect sizes. The one Arm-A/B unit
267: whose rewrite kept dying (`claude exited with code 1`) turned out to be the
268: human side of the `spok` (spoken) register, which in HAP-E is degraded ASR
269: output — word salad, not writing:
270: 
271: > "No clue boots it on the live racist statue poor Lenny versus do something are
272: > the land upon density is it has unique Su Chuan be a playground party..."
273: 
274: Its AI counterpart is ordinary, coherent AI prose. So the `spok` cell does not
275: compare AI writing against human writing; it compares AI writing against a
276: corrupted transcript. Two consequences visible in the data:
277: 
278: - the judges split 86 (gpt) vs 0 (gemini) on that single passage — the widest
279:   disagreement anywhere in the pilot, and enough on its own to drag alpha down;
280: - its rewrite fails reproducibly (probably a safety filter on the garbled text),
281:   so the unit has no rewrite condition at all.
282: 
283: The construct under study is *the AI-likeness of prose*. It is undefined for a
284: passage that is not prose. `spok` is therefore excluded from Arms A and B.
285: (HAP-E's other spoken register, `tvm`, is coherent dialogue and stays in.)
286: 
287: **This is a post-hoc exclusion and is treated as one.** The criterion is a
288: property of the corpus, checkable without reference to any outcome, and it was
289: found through a crash rather than through inspecting effects. Even so, the
290: results report **both** analyses: primary (excluding `spok`) and sensitivity
291: (including it). If the two disagree, the disagreement is the finding.
292: 
293: Arm C is unaffected — its registers are drawn from the ko intake, which has no
294: `spok` cell.
295: 
296: ## Study 1 (main study) — registered 2026-07-10, before any Study 1 data
297: 
298: Study 0 cleared the RQ1 gate (α 0.82/0.67/0.86, all arms above 0.4), so the main
299: study proceeds. This section fixes its design before the first corpus row or
300: judge call exists. Study 0's four deviations are inherited as design inputs, not
301: re-litigated.
302: 
303: ### Arms
304: 
305: Study 1 is powered for **document length only** — the pilot already measured the
306: snippet penalty (α 0.82 → 0.67; effect −14 vs −22), so snippet arms are dropped
307: rather than re-run underpowered.
308: 
309: - **Arm A1 — en, document.** HAP-E paired on `prompt_id`, same substrate and
310:   filters as pilot Arm A (`spok` excluded per Deviation 4, sensitivity restored).
311:   Target n = 25 AI + 25 human (paired), disjoint from the pilot's items where the
312:   register spread allows.
313: - **Arm D — ko, document.** The pilot's blocking prerequisite, built new:
314:   - **Human side:** full-article documents (not paragraph snippets) from the 39
315:     vetted public ko sources in `artifacts/rebaseline-2025/sources.ko-public.jsonl`
316:     (5 registers). A document = consecutive accepted paragraphs of one page,
317:     ≥ 3 paragraphs, 1 200–4 000 chars. Raw text stays gitignored/private,
318:     hash-only redistribution — same policy as every prior corpus here.
319:   - **AI side, topic-paired:** for each human document, ONE AI document generated
320:     from its **public title + register + length band only** (the human text never
321:     enters the prompt), so topic and register are controlled by construction, as
322:     in HAP-E. Generation family rotates deterministically across
323:     gpt (codex CLI) / claude (claude CLI) / moonshot (kimi CLI) / xai (grok API),
324:     giving ~6–7 documents per family. claude-generated items are legitimate:
325:     the rewriter-family-vs-judge control lives in the panel, not the corpus.
326: - Target cell size: 20–30 (power from pilot Δ SDs); anything below 20 in a cell
327:   is reported as underpowered, not silently pooled.
328: 
329: ### Judge panel (fixed up front, per the pilot's go/no-go conditions)
330: 
331: - **Panel: `judge-kimi` (moonshot) + `judge-gpt` (codex) + `judge-grok` (xai).**
332:   All three are cross-family to the rewriter (claude-cli, unchanged).
333: - **gemini is excluded before the first call**: its API project exhausted the
334:   monthly spending cap (429 RESOURCE_EXHAUSTED, probed 2026-07-10). This is a
335:   scheduled-recovery outage, not a judging decision; recorded here so panel
336:   composition cannot quietly follow quota luck mid-run, which is exactly what
337:   Deviation 3 was.
338: - **2-of-3 quorum:** a passage's panel score = mean of its parseable primary
339:   ratings; a passage-condition with < 2 ratings after top-up is reported as a
340:   data loss, never scored from a single judge.
341: - Quota headroom is probed on all three backends (a real judge-format call, not
342:   a ping) immediately before the recorded run; the probe results go in the run
343:   log.
344: 
345: ### Outcomes (fixed)
346: 
347: 1. **H2a (primary, per arm):** panel AI-likeness Δ on AI texts, paired, Cliff's
348:    δ + 95% bootstrap CI — same rule as Study 0.
349: 2. **H2b:** AI-call rate on rewritten AI text < half the original's rate.
350: 3. **H6 (new primary — structural-tell survival).** The pilot's headline was
351:    that surviving cues are architectural. Study 1 pre-registers it: each judge
352:    `strongest_cue` on a REWRITTEN AI passage is classified by a **deterministic
353:    keyword rubric fixed in the harness before any data** into
354:    `structure / lexical / specificity-absence / other`.
355:    - H6: `structure` is the **modal category** of surviving cues at document
356:      length, in both arms.
357:    - The rubric is code, not judgment: cue strings are matched case-insensitively
358:      against fixed keyword lists committed with the harness; unmatched cues fall
359:      to `other` and are listed verbatim in the results.
360: 4. **RQ4 (house-style fingerprint)** re-run at n≈25: permutation test as in the
361:    pilot, now adequately powered for a directional answer.
362: 5. **RQ5a/RQ5b** unchanged (gate pass-rate ≥ 95%; human-control collateral).
363: 6. **Anti-circularity rule** unchanged and evaluated per arm.
364: 
365: ### What Study 1 does NOT claim
366: 
367: - zh/ja: no document-length corpus exists in either language; out of scope,
368:   carried as a named gap exactly as ko was in Study 0.
369: - Modern-model coverage in Arm A1 is still bounded by HAP-E's single 2024
370:   generator; Arm D's rotated families carry the modern-model claim.
371: - Judge identity differs from Study 0 (gemini+kimi → kimi+codex+grok), so
372:   absolute score levels are not comparable across studies; only within-study
373:   paired deltas are interpreted.
374: 
375: ## Study 2 (intervention study) — registered 2026-07-12, before any Study 2 data
376: 
377: Study 1's headline was that Korean document rewrites barely move perception
378: (Δ −6.0) and that 75–81% of surviving cues are structural. The
379: `ko-doc-structure` pack (6 document-architecture patterns, private pro pack,
380: authored 2026-07-10 from Study 1's cue evidence) is the designed response.
381: Study 2 measures whether it actually works — before any efficacy claim ships
382: in marketing or pack docs.
383: 
384: ### Design
385: 
386: - **Intervention:** the `ko-doc-structure` pack installed into
387:   `custom/patterns/` (via the shipping `patina pack` path), which places its 6
388:   patterns into the live rewrite prompt. Everything else — corpus, rewriter
389:   (claude-cli), judge panel, prompts, analyzer stats — is identical to
390:   Study 1's Arm D.
391: - **Arm D2:** re-rewrite the SAME 54 Study 1 ko documents (27 AI + 27 human,
392:   by stored original text) with the pack active; judge each new rewrite with
393:   the same fixed 3-judge panel (kimi / gpt-5.5 / grok-4.5, 2-of-3 quorum).
394:   Originals are NOT re-judged: Study 1's original-condition ratings are reused
395:   as the shared baseline, so the paired comparison is
396:   Δ2(doc) = panel(rewrite₂) − panel(original₁) vs Δ1(doc) from Study 1.
397: - **Primary outcome (H-S2a):** paired per-document improvement
398:   d(doc) = Δ2 − Δ1 on AI documents; hypothesis mean d < 0 (the pack helps),
399:   95% bootstrap CI excluding 0.
400: - **Secondary (H-S2b):** among still-called-"ai" judgments on D2 rewrites, the
401:   structural share of strongest-cues (same fixed rubric) falls below 60%
402:   (Study 1: 81%).
403: - **Guard rails:** RQ5a gate pass-rate ≥ 95% must hold (structure edits are
404:   the riskiest for meaning); human-control Δ2 must not worsen beyond Study 1's
405:   band (over-editing check); rewrite length ratio reported.
406: - **Confound noted up front:** rewriter and judges are stochastic and two days
407:   have passed; the comparison is same-original paired but not same-run
408:   randomized. If d is small relative to rewrite-rerun noise, the honest read
409:   is "cannot distinguish from rerun variance" — a no-effect verdict, not a
410:   spin. (A same-run A/B was rejected for cost; this is a pilot-grade
411:   intervention estimate.)
412: - Quota headroom probed on all four backends immediately before the run
413:   (done 2026-07-12: codex, kimi, grok, claude all answered).
414: 
415: ### What ships on the result
416: 
417: - d meaningfully negative → the pack's efficacy line in PRO-PACKS/marketing
418:   may cite it, with CIs and this registration.
419: - d ≈ 0 or positive → published as-is in the results doc; the pack docs must
420:   NOT claim measured efficacy, and the next iteration targets whatever the
421:   surviving cues say.
422: 
423: ## Study 3 (structural-mechanism intervention) — registered 2026-07-12, before any Study 3 data
424: 
425: Study 2 rejected the prompt-text mechanism: describing document-architecture
426: patterns to the rewriter produced no perceptual improvement (paired d −0.7
427: [−3.4, +1.8]) and violated both guard rails (meaning gate 87.0%, all failures
428: dropped numbers; human-control over-editing d +5.1 [+0.3, +9.6]). Its results
429: doc names the design implication: structural humanization needs a
430: **structural mechanism**, not more prompt prose. Study 3 tests the first
431: candidate on that list — a pre-rewrite **structure plan step**.
432: 
433: ### Design
434: 
435: - **Intervention — two-stage rewrite (plan → execute), same rewriter
436:   (claude-cli) for both stages:**
437:   1. **Plan stage:** the model receives the original document plus a fixed
438:      planning instruction and outputs a reorganization plan: explicit
439:      merge/split/reorder decisions, target paragraph-size asymmetry, which
440:      checklist/parallel structures to dismantle — under hard constraints
441:      written into the plan contract: every number, date, and named entity is
442:      enumerated in the plan and marked KEEP-verbatim; no new content units;
443:      no plan may delete a content unit (S2's failure mode was compression
444:      dropping facts).
445:   2. **Execute stage:** the model receives the original plus the plan and
446:      produces the rewrite by carrying out the plan. The plan text is recorded
447:      per row alongside the rewrite (auditable intermediate).
448: - **Arm D3:** re-rewrite the SAME 54 Study 1 ko documents (27 AI + 27 human,
449:   by stored original text). Panel, quorum, prompts, analyzer stats identical
450:   to Study 1/2 (kimi / gpt-5.5 / grok-4.5, 2-of-3). Originals are not
451:   re-judged: Study 1 original ratings remain the shared baseline, so
452:   d(doc) = Δ3 − Δ1, with Δ2 available as a descriptive second comparator only.
453: - **Primary outcome (H-S3a):** mean paired d on AI documents negative with
454:   95% bootstrap CI excluding 0 AND point estimate ≤ −5. The stricter point
455:   threshold is deliberate: the mechanism doubles per-document rewrite cost
456:   (2 LLM calls), so a sub-5-point gain does not justify shipping it.
457: - **Secondary (H-S3b):** structural share of strongest-cues among
458:   still-called-"ai" D3 judgments < 60% (S1: 81%, S2: 82%).
459: - **Guard rails (both must hold; a violation blocks shipping regardless of d):**
460:   1. meaning-safety gate pass ≥ 95% (≥ 52/54) — the direct test of the
461:      KEEP-verbatim plan contract against S2's dropped-numbers failure;
462:   2. human-control paired d must NOT be significantly positive (CI includes 0
463:      or is negative) — no repeat of S2's measured over-editing harm.
464:   Rewrite/original length ratio is reported per class (S2 mean 0.91).
465: - **Confound noted up front:** same as Study 2 — rewriter/judges stochastic,
466:   runs days apart; paired design absorbs document-level variance, not
467:   run-level drift. Near-zero d again reads as "indistinguishable from rerun
468:   variance", not spin.
469: - Runner: extends the Study 2 harness (fail-soft recorded rows,
470:   resume-by-original_sha); quota headroom on all judge backends probed
471:   immediately before the run, per S2 practice.
472: 
473: ### What ships on the result
474: 
475: - All criteria pass → the plan-step mechanism graduates to a **product
476:   proposal** (separate approval; candidate surfaces: a restructure option in
477:   the rewrite pipeline or a redesigned pro pack v2 that carries the plan
478:   contract) citing this registration and the measured CIs.
479: - H-S3a fails or a guard rail is violated → published as-is; next iteration
480:   falls to candidate 2 from the Study 2 results doc (deterministic structure
481:   transforms with LLM infill). No efficacy claim ships from a failed study.
482: 
483: No Study 3 corpus row, rewrite, plan, or judgment exists at registration time.
484:

487: - TH-Bench (humanizing attacks vs detectors) — arXiv:2503.08708
488: - Paraphrasing evades detectors (DIPPER) — arXiv:2303.13408
````
